 succeeded in 0ms:
# Research Target

Let \(p\) be prime and let \(d\) be an integer satisfying \(100<d<p\). The dimension is fixed at \(m=2\). Degrees \(d\le 100\), including the degenerate case \(d=0\), are outside the campaign scope and must not be treated as obstructions to the target. A point table is a function

\[
f:\mathbb F_p^2\to\mathbb F_p.
\]

For every affine line \(L\subseteq\mathbb F_p^2\), a line table supplies a univariate polynomial \(P_L\) of degree at most \(d\), interpreted on \(L\). Define the test agreement

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)
=\Pr_{L,\,x\in L}[P_L(x)=f(x)],
\]

where \(L\) is uniform among affine lines and \(x\) is uniform on \(L\).

## Current benchmark

Kominers--Thaler--Zheng prove that there are absolute constants \(C,c>0\) such that

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)\ge C(d/p)^{1/3}
\]

implies the existence of a total-degree-at-most-\(d\) polynomial \(Q\) satisfying

\[
\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\ge c\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P).
\]

## Target theorem

The campaign seeks progress toward the following deliberately explicit research target.

For every fixed \(\eta>0\), prove constants \(C_\eta,c_\eta>0\) and a fully stated admissible parameter regime with \(100<d<p\) such that

\[
\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)
\ge C_\eta(d/p)^{1-\eta}
\]

implies

\[
\max_{\deg Q\le d}\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]
\ge c_\eta\operatorname{Agr}_{\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P).
\]

Equivalent or stronger list-decoding conclusions are admissible only when the conversion to a single polynomial and its loss are proved.

## Milestones

- **Bivariate exponent improvement:** replace \(1/3\) by an explicit \(\alpha>1/3\) over prime fields.
- **Combinatorial improvement:** strengthen the incidence/concentration stage with its full parameter range.
- **Algebraic improvement:** strengthen the interpolation/factorization stage with complete prime-characteristic handling.
- **Near-linear exponent:** prove \((d/p)^{1-\eta}\) for every fixed \(\eta>0\).
- **Sharp threshold:** identify matching examples or prove a theorem at \(\Theta(d/p)\), if true.

Because \(d/p<1\), a larger exponent is a stronger result: it permits smaller local agreement.

The general-dimensional lift is standard once the bivariate statement is known. It is a downstream corollary, not a research milestone, and the campaign spends no agents on it.

## Lemma writing rule

**A lemma statement contains only its quantified objects, hypotheses, and conclusion. It contains no motivation, derivation, commentary, proof sketch, interpretation, history, or explanation; all such material belongs in the proof.**

The dedicated Lemma Writer post-edits every structured proof step without changing its mathematical content or status. It may split a source lemma into ordered pieces when that makes the logical structure easier to understand. Raw submissions remain immutable, and every edited statement is source-linked and checked deterministically with KaTeX before publication in the Lemma Book.

## Proof roadmap policy

Three synchronized roadmap agents maintain complete dependency DAGs for the algebraic engine, incidence engine, and end-to-end soundness chain. They share all submissions, audits, Lemma Book entries, peer roadmaps, and informal messages; each gives a majority of its attention to its assigned route. Roadmap progress is harness-derived. A node is verified only when it cites an exact source job, proof-step id, and response hash whose claim was independently accepted, and all of its dependencies are verified or external inputs. Agent work states and message-board claims never confer verification.

## Non-results

The following do not count as achieving an exponent milestone:

- a theorem only for fixed \(d\), unless clearly labeled;
- a hidden assumption \(p\ge d^C\) that makes the stated threshold vacuous;
- a list of many global polynomials without a proved single-polynomial conclusion;
- an exponent obtained after suppressing characteristic- or logarithmic losses;
- numerical success on small fields;
- a proof for line-versus-line, plane-versus-point, or axis-parallel tests presented as a line-versus-point theorem without a proved reduction.
- a result whose substantive advance concerns \(m\ne2\), extension fields, or dimension bootstrapping rather than the bivariate prime-field theorem.

  "note_markdown": "# The bivariate prime-field cubic bottleneck: support sensitivity and a modal obstruction\n\n## Abstract\n\nFor the affine line-versus-point test on `F_p²`, the KTZ cubic threshold comes from one precise chain:\n\n`|R| = O(p/ε)`, `D = Θ(sqrt(d|R|))`, and `D = O(εp)`.\n\nThe first relation is the selected-line cost, the second is weighted interpolation, and the third is simultaneously required for identity extension, deletion of nonsimple incidences, and root lifting. Their combination is `ε³ = Ω(d/p)`.\n\nRetaining the fraction σ of lines supporting an accepted carrier of edge mass δ gives the sharper proved relation `δ³ = Ω(dσ/p)`. In particular, σ=O(δ) gives a conditional exponent `1/2`, but not an unconditional benchmark improvement. In the prime regime, I also prove that the primitive squarefree interpolant satisfies `gcd(B,B_Z)=1`, so KTZ's generic directional derivative can be replaced by the vertical derivative.\n\nFinally, I give a legal obstruction at ambient degree `d=101`: modal constant line labels can attain local agreement `Θ(log p/(p log log p))` while every total-degree-at-most-101 polynomial has agreement below `2/p`. Thus a uniform exact threshold `C d/p` with global agreement `Ω(ε)` is false. This obstruction remains below every fixed-η trigger `(d/p)^(1−η)`.\n\n## Test and Notation\n\nFix a prime `p` and an integer `101≤d<p`; the dimension is exactly two. Let `L` range uniformly over the `p(p+1)` affine lines of `F_p²`, and then let `x` range uniformly over the `p` points of `L`. This is uniform sampling from the `p²(p+1)` incidences and is equivalent to first choosing `x` uniformly and then an incident line uniformly.\n\nThe point table is an arbitrary function `f:F_p²→F_p`. Each line has a univariate polynomial label `P_L` of degree at most `d`. All global degrees below are total degrees. Define\n\n`ε = Pr_{L,x∈L}[P_L(x)=f(x)]`\n\nand\n\n`Agr_d(f) = max_{Q: total-degree(Q)≤d} Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]`.\n\nAn accepted carrier is any set `E*` of accepted incidences. Write\n\n`δ = |E*|/[p²(p+1)]`,  `T = projection of E* to lines`,  `σ = |T|/[p(p+1)]`.\n\nNecessarily `0<δ≤σ≤1`. Taking all accepted incidences gives `δ=ε`; a carrier with `δ≥ε/2` retains a constant fraction of the test acceptance.\n\n## Prior Results\n\nThe primary benchmark is [Kominers–Thaler–Zheng, ECCC TR26-147, Revision 1](https://eccc.weizmann.ac.il/report/2026/147/revision/1/download/). Its Theorem 1.1, specialized to the present regime, states that absolute constants `C,c>0` give global agreement at least `cε` when `ε≥C(d/p)^(1/3)`. The theorem statement hides the constants. The bivariate proof in Sections 3–6 explicitly closes with recovery `ε/8000`.\n\nThe source chooses `k0=floor(εp/100)`, `k1=floor(εp/1000)`, a selected line set of size `O(p/ε)`, and\n\n`D = 16(floor(sqrt(d|R|))+1)`.\n\nRevision 1 uses a squarefree primitive polynomial, a generic directional derivative, and a resultant; it does not use a discriminant in the bivariate proof. The constant `10^15` below is my conservative reconstruction for a support-sensitive variant, not a constant quoted from Theorem 1.1. The older cached base report and Revision 1 must not be conflated.\n\nThe Newton step is consistent with [Harsha–Kumar–Saptharishi–Sudan, arXiv:2311.12752v1](https://arxiv.org/abs/2311.12752), especially Lemma 2.10, but the needed lifting argument is reproduced below. No Arora–Sudan theorem is used as a black box.\n\nThe active durable corpus was also audited. All eight active leaderboard files were empty, and there were no completed submissions or verifier-audit files to inherit. The [data manifest](/Users/kaizheng/Documents/ChatGPT/Line-vs-Point/research_state/DATA_MANIFEST.json) is a pre-launch snapshot relative to the live campaign state, so it was treated as provenance rather than mathematical evidence. Every directory named `superseded` was excluded.\n\n## Theorem\n\n**Theorem A — support-sensitive KTZ closure.** Let `p` be prime, `101≤d<p`, and let an accepted carrier have parameters `(δ,σ)`. Put `K=10^15`. If\n\n`δ³ ≥ K dσ/p`,\n\nthen there is a polynomial `Q∈F_p[X,Y]` of total degree at most `d` with\n\n`Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)] ≥ δ/8000`.\n\nThus, if `δ≥ε/2`, the conclusion is at least `ε/16000`. With all accepted edges and σ≤1, the sufficient threshold is `ε≥10^5(d/p)^(1/3)`. Under the additional structural condition `σ≤Aδ`, it becomes the conditional square-root threshold `δ≥sqrt(10^15 A d/p)`.\n\n**Lemma B — prime vertical derivative.** If `B∈F_p[X,Y,Z]` is squarefree, primitive as a polynomial in `Z`, and has total degree below `p`, then `gcd(B,B_Z)=1`.\n\n**Theorem C — modal obstruction at the legal lower boundary.** Let `d=101`, `N=(d+1)(d+2)/2=5253`, and\n\n`r=floor(log p/(4 log log p))`.\n\nFor every sufficiently large prime, explicitly every prime `p≥max{⌈e^32⌉,100N²}`, there are a point table and constant line labels, hence labels of degree at most 101, such that\n\n`r/(130p) ≤ ε ≤ 50r/p`,  while  `Agr_101(f)<2/p`.\n\nConsequently, for every fixed `C,c>0`, arbitrarily large primes admit `ε≥C·101/p` but `Agr_101(f)<cε`.\n\n**Proposition D — near-p information baseline.** For every `101≤d<p` and every point table,\n\n`Agr_d(f) ≥ (d+1)(d+2)/(2p²)`.\n\n## Proof or Conditional Proof\n\n**[P1] (proved) Carrier pruning.** Set `k0=floor(δp/100)`. Iteratively delete point vertices and line vertices whose current carrier degree is below `k0`. Point deletions remove at most `p²k0` edges and line deletions at most `p(p+1)k0` edges. In normalized incidence measure the loss is at most\n\n`k0/(p+1)+k0/p < δ/50`.\n\nKeep the weaker bound that the remaining graph `G0` has edge mass at least `δ/4`. It has minimum degree `k0`; its line set `L0` has normalized measure λ satisfying `δ/4≤λ≤σ`.\n\n**[P2] (proved) Support-sensitive selector.** Select each line of `L0` independently with probability `θ=8/k0`. The hypothesis and `σ≥δ` imply\n\n`δ²≥10^15 d/p`,  `δp≥sqrt(10^15dp)>3·10^9`,\n\nso `k0≥δp/200`. For a fixed `L∈L0`, coverage indicators at distinct neighbors of `L` depend on disjoint sets of other lines. Each has success probability above `3/4`; Hoeffding therefore bounds failure to cover half the neighbors by `exp(−k0/8)`. Since `k0≥8 log(4p(p+1))`, a union bound over all affine lines costs less than `1/4`.\n\nIf `r=|R|`, then `E[r]=θ|L0|≥200(p+1)` and `Var(r)≤E[r]`. Chebyshev puts `r` between half and twice its mean with failure below `1/4`. Some deterministic selection consequently satisfies both coverage and\n\n`100(p+1)≤r≤16|L0|/k0≤6400σp/δ`.\n\nThese are the only Hoeffding, selector union, and Chebyshev losses.\n\n**[P3] (proved) Weighted interpolation.** Give `X,Y,Z` weights `1,1,d` and set\n\n`D=16(floor(sqrt(dr))+1)`.\n\nThen `D²>256dr` and KTZ's monomial count gives `dim(V_D)≥D³/(64d)>r(D+1)`. Each formal identity `A(L(t),P_L(t))=0` imposes at most `D+1` coefficient equations, so a nonzero interpolant exists. Moreover,\n\n`D≤1280 sqrt(dσp/δ)+16≤1280δp/sqrt(10^15)+16<δp/10000`.\n\nThus `D<p`, `D<k0/2`, and, for `k1=floor(δp/1000)`, `D<k1/2`. Also `r>D`. A nonzero `Z`-independent polynomial of degree at most `D` cannot vanish identically on more than `D` affine lines, so `A_Z≠0`. Choosing minimum weighted degree removes repeated factors and makes `A` squarefree.\n\n**[P4] (proved) Identity extension and formal content removal.** Every `L∈L0` has more than `k0/2>D` distinct accepted points that also lie on selected accepted lines. At each such point the two line labels equal the same value `f(x)`. Hence the degree-at-most-`D` polynomial `A(L(t),P_L(t))` has more than `D` distinct roots and is identically zero.\n\nWrite `A=H(X,Y)B(X,Y,Z)`, where `H` is the gcd of the coefficients in `Z`. At most `deg(H)≤D` base lines satisfy `H|_L=0` identically. On every other retained line, `H(L(t))B(L(t),P_L(t))=0` in the integral domain `F_p[t]`; hence `B(L(t),P_L(t))=0`. This is formal polynomial division, not division by values of `H`. No root multiplicities are counted.\n\n**[P5] (proved) Prime vertical-derivative lemma.** Factor the squarefree primitive polynomial as `B=c∏P_i` into distinct irreducibles over `F_p`. Primitivity implies that every `P_i` genuinely depends on `Z`. Since `deg_Z(P_i)<p`, its derivative `(P_i)_Z` is nonzero, and its smaller degree implies `P_i` does not divide `(P_i)_Z`. Modulo `P_i`,\n\n`B_Z = (P_i)_Z ∏_{j≠i}P_j ≠0`.\n\nThus no irreducible factor of `B` divides `B_Z`, proving `gcd(B,B_Z)=1`. No irreducibility over the algebraic closure is required. This replaces, in the prime-field regime only, KTZ's generic directional derivative.\n\n**[P6] (proved) Degenerate-root deletion.** Apply KTZ's resultant line bound to the coprime pair `(B,B_Z)`. Delete the at most `D` content lines, at most `D(D−1)` lines on which the two graph identities degenerate together, and at most `D` zeros of `B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))` on every other line. Their total normalized edge mass is bounded by\n\n`D/[p(p+1)] + D(D−1)/[p(p+1)] + D/p < δ/16`.\n\nPruning both sides at degree `k1=floor(δp/1000)` costs at most\n\n`k1/(p+1)+k1/p<δ/500`.\n\nThe surviving graph `G1` has edge mass at least `δ/8`, minimum degree `k1`, and `B_Z(x,f(x))≠0` at every surviving point. No discriminant or Markov inequality appears.\n\n**[P7] (proved) Hensel lifting and propagation.** At a surviving point translate the base coordinates to `(0,0)` and put `α=f(0,0)`. In `F_p[[X,Y]]`, construct a root `Φ=α+Φ_1+Φ_2+…` recursively by homogeneous degree. At stage `j`, the degree-`j` residual is canceled uniquely by division by the scalar `B_Z(0,0,α)`. Thus no factorial is inverted.\n\nEvery incident line polynomial is the same unique root after restriction to that line. There are more than `D` incident directions. Therefore every homogeneous part `Φ_j` with `d<j≤D` vanishes on more than `D≥j` projective directions and is zero. Let `Q` be the truncation through degree `d`. The polynomial `B(X,Y,Q)` has degree at most `D` and all terms through degree `D` vanish, so it is identically zero. Simple-root uniqueness propagates the same `Q` across every edge of the connected component, and `Q=f` at all of its point vertices.\n\n**[P8] (proved) Component mixing and recovery.** For a component let `a,b,e` denote its normalized point, line, and edge measures. Put `κ=k1/p` and `u=1/sqrt(p+1)`. Minimum degree and point-line mixing give\n\n`e≥κa/2`,  `e≥κb`,  and  `e≤ab+u sqrt(ab)`.\n\nThe theorem hypothesis gives `u<δ/8000≤κ/4`. If `a<κ/4`, the last two inequalities imply `9b≤a`, which contradicts the first inequality. If `b<κ/4`, they imply `a≤b`, which contradicts `e≥κb`. Hence `a,b≥κ/4≥δ/8000`. The polynomial from [P7] therefore has global agreement at least `δ/8000`. This is the sole incidence Cauchy–Schwarz loss.\n\n**[P9] (proved) Exact cubic reconstruction.** The dominant scales are\n\n`r=O(σp/δ)`,  `D=O(sqrt(dσp/δ))`,  `D=O(δp)`.\n\nThe first two give the interpolation degree; the last is independently demanded by identity extension, the `D/p` bad-incidence term, and lifting. Squaring the final comparison yields exactly `δ³=Ω(dσ/p)`. The resultant term `D²/p²` alone needs only `δ²=Ω(dσ/p)`, and mixing needs only `δ=Ω(p^−1/2)`; both are weaker here. For σ=1 this is the KTZ cubic exponent.\n\n**[P10] (conditional) Concentrated-carrier exponent.** If additionally `σ≤Aδ`, [P9] becomes `δ²=Ω(Ad/p)`, giving exponent `1/2`. Since every carrier has `σ≥δ`, this support-sensitive interpolation balance cannot exceed exponent `1/2`. This is not an unconditional improvement of the benchmark.\n\n**[P11] (proved) Modal occupancy lower bound.** For Theorem C choose all `p²` point values independently and uniformly. On a fixed line let `N_a` be the occupancy of value `a`, `M=max_a N_a`, and `Y=Σ_a 1[N_a=r]`. For one value,\n\n`Pr[N_a=r] = binom(p,r)p^(−r)(1−1/p)^(p−r) ≥ 1/(8r!)`.\n\nHere `r²≤p`, `(1−1/p)^p≥1/4`, and `r!≤r^r≤p^(1/4)`. Thus `μ=E[Y]≥p^(3/4)/8≥1`. For distinct values, the joint probability of both occupancies equaling `r` is at most `1/(r!)²`, at most 64 times the product of the lower bounds. Hence `E[Y²]≤μ+64μ²≤65μ²`. Cauchy–Schwarz, equivalently the zero-threshold Paley–Zygmund argument, gives `Pr[Y>0]≥1/65`, so `E[M]≥r/65`.\n\nGive each line a modal constant label. By linearity of expectation across lines—no line independence is asserted—the local agreement `A` satisfies `E[A]≥r/(65p)`.\n\n**[P12] (proved) Modal upper concentration.** A factorial-moment union bound gives\n\n`Pr[M≥s]≤p/s!≤p(e/s)^s`.\n\nFor `s0=ceil(4 log p/log log p)`, the tail sums to less than one and `E[M]≤s0+1≤25r`; hence `E[A]≤25r/p`. Changing one point value changes the modal count on only its `p+1` incident lines and changes `A` by at most `1/p²`. McDiarmid therefore gives\n\n`Pr[A>50r/p]≤exp(−1250r²)≤r/(520p)`.\n\nNo Markov inequality is used.\n\n**[P13] (proved) Global union bound and deterministic instance.** There are `p^N` formal total-degree-at-most-101 polynomials, with `N=5253`. For fixed `Q`, its agreement count with random `f` is `Bin(p²,1/p)`, of mean `p`. Chernoff gives\n\n`Pr[agreements(Q,f)≥2p]≤(e/4)^p`.\n\nUnion over all `p^N` polynomials gives `p^N(e/4)^p≤exp(−p/4)≤r/(520p)` under the stated lower bound on `p`. Let `G` be the event that all global agreements are below `2/p`, and `H={A≤50r/p}`. Since `0≤A≤1`,\n\n`E[A·1_{G∩H}] ≥ r/(65p)−r/(520p)−r/(520p) > r/(130p)`.\n\nSome deterministic table in `G∩H` therefore has all three bounds in Theorem C.\n\n**[P14] (refuted) Uniform exact-linear soundness.** Suppose constants `C,c>0` made `ε≥Cd/p` imply `Agr_d(f)≥cε` uniformly. Fix the legal ambient cap `d=101` and choose a sufficiently large prime with `r≥max{130·101C,260/c}`. Theorem C then has `ε≥C·101/p` but `Agr_101(f)<2/p<cε`, a contradiction. Thus the exact-linear statement is false. On the other hand, for each fixed `η>0`,\n\n`ε/(101/p)^(1−η) ≤ 50r/[101^(1−η)p^η] → 0`,\n\nso this construction does not refute a fixed-η target.\n\n**[P15] (proved) Near-p information set.** Choose distinct `a_0,…,a_d` and `b_0,…,b_d` and the triangular set `S={(a_i,b_j):i+j≤d}`. The Newton products `∏_{h<i}(X−a_h)∏_{k<j}(Y−b_k)` with `i+j≤d` form a triangular evaluation basis on `S`. Hence arbitrary prescribed values on all `|S|=(d+1)(d+2)/2` points extend to a total-degree-at-most-`d` polynomial. Applying this to `f|_S` proves Proposition D. In particular, the lower bound is `(p+1)/(2p)` at `d=p−1` and `5253/10609` at `d=101,p=103`.\n\n**[P16] (proved) Diffuse support need not concentrate.** Fix the legal ambient `d≥101`, take `f=0`, and on every line choose a degree-`d` label with `d` distinct prescribed parameter roots. Then `ε=d/p` and every line supports acceptance, so `σ=1`; nevertheless `Q=0` agrees globally everywhere. Thus local density does not imply `σ=O(ε)`, although this example is harmless for soundness.\n\n**[P17] (refuted as an obstruction) Concentrated good directions.** Let `f` be a product of `d+1` distinct homogeneous linear forms. In each of the corresponding `d+1` projective directions, every affine-line restriction loses its degree-`d+1` leading term and is a legal degree-`d` label. This gives local mass about `(d+1)/(p+1)`. But `Q=0` agrees on the union of the same `d+1` lines through the origin, a Θ(d/p) fraction. The attempted counterexample already contains the required global polynomial.\n\n**[P18] (refuted safety of omitting simplicity) Branch collision.** For odd `p` and legal ambient cap `d≥101`, `B=Z²−X²` is squarefree but its two legal branches `Q=X` and `Q=−X` meet at the origin, where `B_Z=0`. Agreement at that incidence cannot select a branch. Vertical-simple-root deletion is therefore logically necessary.\n\n**[P19] (refuted as an active obstruction) Inseparability.** A polynomial with vanishing `Z`-derivative must use positive `Z`-exponents divisible by `p`. Since the interpolant has weighted degree `D<p`, no such exponent occurs. Examples such as `Z^p−G` lie outside the interpolation space and do not obstruct the prime-field proof.\n\n## Exponent Ledger\n\n| Stage | Scale or loss | Consequence |\n|---|---|---|\n| Carrier pruning | `<δ/50`, retained as `δ/4` | Constant only |\n| Selector probability | Hoeffding `e^(−k0/8)`, union over `p(p+1)` | No δ-power loss |\n| Selector size | Chebyshev; `r≤6400σp/δ` | One factor `σ/δ` |\n| Interpolation | `dim(V_D)≈D³/d` versus `rD` constraints | `D≈sqrt(dr)` |\n| Root budget | `D≤Θ(δp)` | `δ³=Ω(dσ/p)` |\n| Content/resultant deletion | `D/p + D²/p²` | Cubic plus a weaker quadratic gate |\n| Second pruning | `<δ/500` | Constant only |\n| Mixing | One Cauchy–Schwarz error `p^−1/2` | Weaker than cubic for `d≥101` |\n| Recovery | Component point density `δ/8000` | Linear global recovery |\n| Diffuse carrier | `σ=1` | Exponent `1/3` |\n| Concentrated carrier | `σ≤Aδ` | Conditional exponent `1/2` |\n| Modal occupancy | `r≈log p/(4 log log p)` | Local `Θ(r/p)` |\n| Polynomial union | `p^5253(e/4)^p` | Global `<2/p` |\n| Exact-linear claim | Ratio at most `260/r` | Tends to zero; exponent 1 with constant threshold is false |\n\nThere is no hidden Markov loss. The only probabilistic inequalities are the selector's Hoeffding, union, and Chebyshev bounds; the modal occupancy second moment, factorial union, and McDiarmid bound; and the global Chernoff union. The sole incidence Cauchy–Schwarz loss is the `p^−1/2` mixing error.\n\n## Counterexample Attempts\n\nThe successful adversarial table is Theorem C: it is diffuse across lines and exploits the maximum occupancy among `p` symbols, not low characteristic or a small exceptional direction set. It proves that the campaign's possible sharp threshold cannot literally be a constant multiple of `d/p` with linear recovery.\n\nThe all-lines prescribed-root table [P16] shows why line-support concentration cannot be assumed. The homogeneous-direction construction [P17] fails because its zero set already supplies a global polynomial. The branch collision [P18] validates the simple-root deletion. The inseparable attempt [P19] is excluded by `D<p`. Proposition D handles `d` near `p`; it is a positive agreement baseline, not an obstruction.\n\n## Characteristic Audit\n\n- The field is exactly the prime field `F_p`; no extension-field descent is used.\n- The ambient degree always satisfies `101≤d<p`. Constant or linear objects appearing inside examples are legal labels or branches under that ambient cap; no test with parameter `d≤100` is analyzed.\n- Because `D<p`, the characteristic-pruned weighted monomial set equals the full weighted ball: every positive `Z`-exponent is strictly below `p`.\n- Lemma B rules out inseparable vertical factors after content removal. Absolute irreducibility is unnecessary.\n- KTZ's source proof uses a generic directional derivative. The replacement by `B_Z` is a new prime-field simplification and causes no exponent gain.\n- No discriminant is used. The resultant is invoked only for the proved coprime pair `(B,B_Z)`.\n- Division by `H` occurs only as an integral-domain inference on non-content lines. Hensel lifting divides only by a nonzero scalar `B_Z(x,f(x))`.\n- All interpolation constraints are coefficient identities, and every later root count uses distinct points or directions rather than multiplicity.\n- When `d` is near `p`, Theorem A's premise is vacuous because `σ≥δ` would force `p≥10^15d`; Proposition D supplies the appropriate direct check.\n\n## Limitations\n\nThe general KTZ benchmark is not improved: the exponent `1/2` conclusion requires the additional hypothesis `σ=O(δ)`, and diffuse carriers can have `σ=1`. The constant `10^15` is deliberately conservative and is not claimed optimal.\n\nThe modal obstruction refutes a uniform constant-times-`d/p` trigger but not any fixed exponent `1−η`. It instead shows that an exponent-1 formulation must include a subpolynomial occupancy allowance, at least on the order of `log p/log log p` when `d=101`.\n\nThe current selector has an upper bound `O(σp/δ)`, whereas covering more than `k0/2` points on one line requires at least `Ω(δp)` distinct witness lines. Closing this gap demands a new combinatorial or algebraic idea. No computation, higher-dimensional lifting, extension field, or individual-degree substitution is used here."
}

researcher-0007:P5.1	researcher-0007	P5	proved	Factorwise cleanup bounds	Let $p$ be prime and let $101\\le d<p$. Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$, respectively. researcher-0007:P7.1	researcher-0007	P7	proved	Strip construction	Let $p$ be prime, and let $d,D$ be integers satisfying\n\n$$\n101\\le d,\n\\qquad\n2d\\le D<p.\n$$\n\nPut $e=D-d$, choose $S\\subseteq\\mathbb F_p$ with $|S|=e$, and define\n\n$$\nH(X)=\\prod_{a\\in S}(X-a),\n\\qquad\nB=Z(Z-H(X)).\n$$\n\nDefine $f:\\mathbb F_p^2\\to\\mathbb F_p$ by\n\n$$\nf(x,y)=\n\\begin{cases}\n0,&x\\in S,\\\\\n1,&x\\notin S,\n\\end{cases}\n$$\n\nand assign the constant label $P_L=0$ to every nonvertical affine line and the constant label $P_L=2$ to every vertical affine line. researcher-0009:P2.1	researcher-0009	P2	proved	Degree-moment envelopes	Let $p$ be prime, let $101\\le d<p$, and let a valid affine line-versus-point table over $\\mathbb F_p^2$ have agreement $\\epsilon$. researcher-0009:P3.1	researcher-0009	P3	proved	Affine carrier	Let $p$ be prime and let $101\\le d<p$. Define\n\n$$S_d=\\{0,1,\\ldots,d-1\\}\\subset\\mathbb F_p,\\qquad f(x,y)=0.$$\n\nFor every $m,b\\in\\mathbb F_p$, parameterize the nonvertical line $L_{m,b}$ by $L_{m,b}(t)=(t,mt+b)$ and assign it the label\n\n$$P_{m,b}(t)=\\prod_{s\\in S_d}(t+m-s).$$\n\nFor every $c\\in\\mathbb F_p$, parameterize the vertical line $V_c$ by $V_c(t)=(c,t)$ and assign it the label\n\n$$P_{V_c}(t)=\\prod_{s\\in S_d}(t-s).$$\n\nThese data form a valid affine line-versus-point table of agreement $\\epsilon_0=d/p$. Every label has formal degree exactly $d$, every affine line has accepted degree $d$, exactly $pd$ points have accepted degree $d+1$, and the remaining $p(p-d)$ points have accepted degree $d$.
researcher-0009:P4.1	researcher-0009	P4	proved	Carrier graph	Let $p$ be prime, let $101\\le d<p$, and let the valid table be the table defined in P3. Its accepted bipartite point-line graph is connected, and every point of $\\mathbb F_p^2$ and every affine line is a nonisolated vertex.
researcher-0009:P5.1	researcher-0009	P5	proved	Carrier moments	Let $p$ be prime, let $101\\le d<p$, and let the table defined in P3 have agreement $\\epsilon_0=d/p$. researcher-0009:P6.1	researcher-0009	P6	proved	Fractional reaching	Let $p$ be prime, let $101\\le d<p$, and let the table defined in P3 have accepted edge set $E$ and agreement $\\epsilon_0=d/p$. Then\n\n$$\\frac{\\theta p(p+1)}{d+1}\\le\\operatorname{OPT}_\\theta\\le\\frac{\\theta p(p+1)}{d-1}.$$

researcher-0010:[P9].1	researcher-0010	[P9]	proved	Template Popularity	Let $T_1,\\ldots,T_R$ be disjoint affine-line families, and let $Q_1,\\ldots,Q_R\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ have total degree at most $d$. researcher-0010:[P11].1	researcher-0010	[P11]	proved	Star-Moment Identities	Let $a_x$ be the accepted degree of $x$ in a deterministic table over $\\mathbb F_p^2$. For $2\\le s\\le p+1$, let $\\Gamma_s$ be the probability that $s$ ordered distinct uniformly sampled lines through a uniformly sampled point all accept there, and write\n\n$$\n(n)_s=n(n-1)\\cdots(n-s+1).\n$$\n\nThen\n\n$$\n\\Gamma_s=\\frac{1}{p^2(p+1)_s}\\sum_x(a_x)_s.\n$$\n\nIf\n\n$$\n\\mu=\\epsilon(p+1)=k+\\theta,\n\\qquad\nk=\\lfloor\\mu\\rfloor,\n\\qquad\n0\\le\\theta<1,\n$$\n\nthen\n\n$$\n\\Gamma_s\\ge\n\\frac{(1-\\theta)(k)_s+\\theta(k+1)_s}{(p+1)_s}.\n$$\n\nFor $s=2$,\n\n$$\n\\Gamma_2=\\epsilon^2-\n\\frac{\\epsilon(1-\\epsilon)}{p}+\n\\frac{\\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)}.\n$$

ROUND 1
SUMMARY: The exact accepted corpus supplies a complete component-level chain from reaching-family selection through weighted interpolation, linewise extension, characteristic-safe cleanup, vertical simplicity, and recovery of one polynomial. Its whole-explainer budgets force a square-root floor, while the audited universal-cover example and provisional uniform-jet calculation rule out two naive improvements. The shortest credible near-linear route is therefore: prove an algebraic-rank-or-decode dichotomy producing a low-weight relation on constant accepted mass; localize constant mass on one low-weight factor or decode cross-factor transitions; apply factorwise polar cleanup; and lift at one high-degree seed point. Once the irreducible factor is $Z-Q$, all of its assigned edges aggregate globally, avoiding both component fragmentation and the $p^{-1/2}$ mixing floor. The rank and heavy-factor nodes remain open, the factorwise polar input is pending audit, and the final theorem is blocked.

Define $$\\mathcal I=\\{(x,L):x\\in L\\text{ and }P_L(x)=f(x)\\},\\qquad \\mu(E)=\\frac{|E|}{p^2(p+1)}$$ for $E\\subseteq\\mathcal I$.	
AE-02	definition	candidate	Weighted graph relations	Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$. Let $$V_D=\\{A\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\\operatorname{wdeg}(A)\\le D\\},\\qquad N_d(D)=\\dim V_D=\\sum_{j=0}^{\\lfloor D/d\\rfloor}\\binom{D-dj+2}{2}.$$ For an affine parametrization $L(t)$, put $\\rho_L(A)=A(L(t),P_L(t))$. A graph relation on $T$ is a nonzero $A$ satisfying $\\rho_L(A)=0$ for every $L\\in T$.	AE-01

AE-13	reduction	candidate	Whole-explainer square-root floor	Within AE-05--AE-12, $$D=\\Theta\\!\\left(\\sqrt{\\frac{d\\tau p^2}{k}}\\right),\n\\qquad\nD<k,$$ so $k^3=\\Omega(d\\tau p^2)$. Since $\\tau\\ge k/(4p)$, this entails $k^2=\\Omega(dp)$. Thus $k=\\Theta(\\epsilon p)$ cannot close this whole-explainer route below $\\epsilon=\\Omega((d/p)^{1/2})$.	AE-05,AE-06,AE-07,AE-10
AE-14	theorem	candidate	Universal-cover obstruction	For suitable integers $K$ there are legal constant-label tables with $1/(2K)\\le\\epsilon\\le2/K$ such that every line family reaching half of every accepted line has $$|R|\\ge\\frac{p}{32\\epsilon},$$ while some constant polynomial $Q$ satisfies $\\Pr[Q=f]\\ge\\epsilon/2$.	AE-01
AE-15	theorem	candidate	Uniform-jet interpolation barrier	Let $s,D$ be integers with $s\\ge1$ and $d\\le D<p$. For $n>D$, define $$C_s(D,d)=\\sum_{a+b<s}\\max(D-a-db+1,0).$$ If raw dimension counting gives $N_d(D)>nC_s(D,d)$ and transfer uses $h$ compatible intersections with $sh>D$, then $$h^2>\\frac{dn}{12}.$$	AE-02
AE-16	definition	candidate	Factor allocation and polar data	Let $B=c\\prod_{i=1}^sC_i$ be primitive and squarefree with irreducible $Z$-dependent factors. Allocate each line satisfying $B(L(t),P_L(t))=0$ to one factor satisfying $C_i(L(t),P_L(t))=0$, obtaining $T=\\bigsqcup_iT_i$. Put $$E_i=\\{(x,L)\\in E:L\\in T_i\\},\n\\quad\n\\mu_i=\\mu(E_i),\n\\quad\n\\Delta_i=\\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),\n\\quad\nr_i=\\deg_Z(C_i),\n\\quad\n\\gamma_i=\\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),$$ and $$\\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\\Delta_i+r_i\\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).$$	AE-02
AE-17	lemma	candidate	Factorwise separability and polar bound	Assume $\\operatorname{wdeg}(B)<p$. Then $\\gcd(C_i,C_{i,Z})=1$. For each $i$, at most $\\Xi_i$ lines of $T_i$ satisfy $C_{i,Z}(L(t),P_L(t))=0$ identically, and every other assigned line has total polar-zero multiplicity at most $\\gamma_i$. Hence the normalized ramified-incidence loss is at most $$\\sum_i\\frac{\\min\\{|T_i|,\\Xi_i\\}}{p(p+1)}+\n\\sum_i\\frac{|T_i|\\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)}.$$	AE-16
AE-18	obligation	open	Near-linear weighted-rank dichotomy	For every fixed $0<\\eta<1$ there are constants $C_\\eta,A_\\eta,a_\\eta>0$ such that the following holds. If $G$ is an $(\\epsilon,a_\\eta)$-core and $$\\epsilon\\ge C_\\eta\\left(\\frac d p\\right)^{1-\\eta},$$ then either one polynomial $Q$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $\\Pr[Q=f]\\ge a_\\eta\\epsilon$, or there are $T'\\subseteq T$, $E'=E\\cap(X\\times T')$, and a squarefree primitive graph relation $B$ of positive $Z$-degree such that $$\\mu(E')\\ge a_\\eta\\epsilon,\n\\qquad\nB(L(t),P_L(t))=0\\quad(L\\in T'),$$ and, for $D=\\operatorname{wdeg}(B)$ and $k=\\lfloor a_\\eta\\epsilon p\\rfloor$, $$D\\le A_\\eta d\\epsilon^{-\\eta/(1-\\eta)}<\\frac{k}{8}<p.$$	AE-02,AE-03
AE-19	obligation	open	Heavy factor or cross-factor decoder	There is $b_\\eta>0$ such that every relation alternative in AE-18 has one of the following outcomes: a polynomial $Q$ of total degree at most $d$ with $\\Pr[Q=f]\\ge b_\\eta\\epsilon$, or an allocated factor $C_i$ satisfying $$\\mu_i\\ge b_\\eta\\epsilon,\n\\qquad\n4\\Delta_i\\le\\min\\{k,\\mu_i(p+1)\\}.$$	AE-16,AE-18
AE-20	lemma	drafting	Factor-local cleanup leaves a lifting seed	Under AE-17 and the factor alternative of AE-19, delete every identically $C_{i,Z}$-ramified line and every incidence $(x,L)$ with $C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))=0$. The remaining factor-class edge set $E_i^{\\mathrm{cl}}$ satisfies $$|E_i^{\\mathrm{cl}}|>p^2\\Delta_i.$$ In the polynomial alternative, retain its stated conclusion.	AE-17,AE-19
AE-21	obligation	drafting	Vinh-free lifting and global aggregation	The alternatives in AE-20 imply that there is one $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ with $$\\Pr_{x\\in\\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge b_\\eta\\epsilon.$$ In the factor alternative, the conclusion follows with $C_i$ associated to $Z-Q$ and $$\\Pr[Q=f]\\ge\\mu_i.$$	AE-12,AE-20
AE-22	theorem	blocked	Near-linear algebraic propagation engine	For every fixed $0<\\eta<1$ there are constants $C_\\eta,c_\\eta,a_\\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, every integer $100<d<p$, and every line-versus-point instance whose accepted graph contains an $(\\epsilon,a_\\eta)$-core, $$\\epsilon\\ge C_\\eta\\left(\\frac d p\\right)^{1-\\eta}$$ implies the existence of one $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\\Pr_{x\\in\\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge c_\\eta\\epsilon.$$	AE-21

ROUND 1
SUMMARY: The exact corpus-mapped component chain gives a conditional exponent-$1/2$ decoder, while component support and universal cover arguments show that the same cardinality-based route cannot reach exponent $1-o(1)$. The proposed critical path replaces small $|R|$ by low labeled-constraint rank: preserve $\Omega_\eta(\epsilon)$ accepted mass and obtain a relation of weighted degree $D=O_\eta(d)$. Then cleanup costs only $O_\eta(d/p)$, simple roots split the graph among $O_\eta(1)$ factors, and one factor can yield one global polynomial with $\Omega_\eta(\epsilon)$ agreement. The main open incidence node is the low-weight sparsify-or-decode dichotomy; factor linearization also needs a fresh exact proof audit.
CRITICAL: IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH -> IE-L-POPULAR-CORE -> IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY -> IE-L-RANK-INTERP -> IE-L-RANK-EXTEND -> IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT -> IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN -> IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE -> IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT -> IE-R-CLOSE -> IE-GOAL
IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH	definition	candidate	Accepted-incidence graph	Let $\\mathcal L_p$ be the $p(p+1)$ affine lines of $\\mathbb F_p^2$ and let $$E_{\\rm acc}=\\{(x,L):x\\in L\\text{ and }P_L(x)=f(x)\\}.$$ Put $\\epsilon=|E_{\\rm acc}|/[p^2(p+1)]$ and $$\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\\max_{\\deg Q\\le d}\\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)].$$ For $G=(X,T,E)\\subseteq E_{\\rm acc}$ define $$e(G)=\\frac{|E|}{p^2(p+1)},\\quad \\mu(G)=\\frac{|X|}{p^2},\\quad \\tau(G)=\\frac{|T|}{p(p+1)},\\quad k(G)=\\operatorname{mindeg}(G).$$	
IE-D-RANK-REACH	definition	candidate	Rank-reaching line family	For $d\\le D<p$, let $$V_D=\\{A\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\\le D\\},\\qquad N_d(D)=\\dim V_D,$$ and $$\\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\\to\\bigoplus_{M\\in R}\\mathbb F_p[t]_{\\le D},\\qquad A\\mapsto\\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\\bigr)_{M\\in R}.$$ For $G=(X,T,E)$ set $$N_G(L)=\\{x:(x,L)\\in E\\},$$ $$\\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)=\\{x\\in N_G(L):\\exists M\\in R\\setminus\\{L\\},\\ (x,M)\\in E\\}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching if $R\\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $\\operatorname{rank}\\Phi_{R,D}<N_d(D)$, and $|\\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)|>D$ for every $L\\in T$.	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
IE-L-POPULAR-CORE	lemma	candidate	Popularity pruning	If an accepted carrier has edge mass $\\delta>0$ and line support at most $\\sigma$, then for $k_0=\\lfloor\\delta p/100\\rfloor$ it contains a subgraph $G_0$ with $$e(G_0)\\ge\\delta/4,\\qquad k(G_0)\\ge k_0,\\qquad \\delta/4\\le\\tau(G_0)\\le\\sigma.$$	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
IE-L-COMP-MEASURE	lemma	candidate	Accepted component measures	Let $G=(X,T,E)$ be connected with minimum degree $k$, line measure $\\tau$, and $C_*=2^{40}$. If $$k^2\\ge C_*dp,\\qquad k^3\\ge C_*d\\tau p^2,$$ then $$\\mu(G)\\ge\\frac{k}{4p},\\qquad \\tau\\ge\\frac{k}{4p}.$$	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE	lemma	candidate	Component-sensitive reaching family	Under `IE-L-COMP-MEASURE`, writing $t=|T|$, there is $R\\subseteq T$ such that $$\\frac{4t}{k}\\le |R|\\le\\frac{16t}{k}$$ and $$|\\operatorname{Reach}_R(L)|>\\frac12\\deg_G(L)$$ for every $L\\in T$. In particular, $|R|=\\Theta(\\tau p^2/k)$.	IE-L-COMP-MEASURE
IE-L-STANDARD-EXPLAIN	lemma	candidate	Standard explainer and extension	Let $r=|R|$ and $$D=16\\bigl(\\lfloor\\sqrt{dr}\\rfloor+1\\bigr).$$ Under the component hypotheses there is a nonzero squarefree $A\\in V_D$ with $A_Z\\not\\equiv0$, $D/k<1/5000$, and $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$$ for every $L\\in T$.	IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE
IE-L-STANDARD-CLEAN	lemma	candidate	Standard simple-root core	Write $A=HB$ with $B$ primitive in $Z$. At most $pD^2+D|T|$ accepted edges are exceptional. Deleting them and peeling below $\\lfloor k/100\\rfloor$ leaves a nonempty graph $G_1$ of minimum degree at least $\\lfloor k/100\\rfloor$ such that $$B(x,f(x))=0,\\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\\ne0$$ at every point vertex. ROUND 1
SUMMARY: Round 1 records the complete exact accepted KTZ component chain in `E2E-04` through `E2E-11`; its available fixed-component refinement reaches only the conditional square-root frontier `E2E-12`. The near-linear route replaces cardinality interpolation with the open bounded-weight rank-or-decode interface `E2E-15`. Its relation branch gives a squarefree global relation, reached-root extension, characteristic-safe cleanup, a positive-mass simple-root core, and only $O_\theta(1)$ factor classes. A heavy factor can then be linearized as $Z-Q$, allowing all of its edges across all components to charge directly to one global polynomial without Vinh mixing. The goal remains blocked on the new incidence dichotomy and exact audits of the strengthened algebraic transfers; the uniform exact exponent-one endpoint is separately refuted.

There are a formal directional derivative $\\mathcal D$ with $\\gcd(B,\\mathcal D B)=1$ and an exceptional edge set such that $$|E_{\\mathrm{exc}}|\\le pD^2+D|T|.$$ Outside this set, all retained line identities for $B$ hold and $\\mathcal D B$ is nonzero at accepted points.	E2E-08,E2E-18
E2E-20	reduction	drafting	Global clean simple-root core	The exceptional normalized mass satisfies $$|E_{\\mathrm{exc}}|/[p^2(p+1)]\\le(D/p)^2+D/p.$$ Delete these edges and iteratively delete vertices of current degree at most $D$. After increasing $C_\\theta$ if necessary, the remaining graph $G_1=(X_1,T_1,E_1)$ satisfies, for $b_\\theta=a_\\theta/2$, $$e(G_1)\\ge b_\\theta\\epsilon,\n\\qquad \\operatorname{mindeg}(G_1)>D.$$ Every retained line has the $B$-identity, and every retained point satisfies $$B(x,f(x))=0,\n\\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\\ne0.$$	E2E-10,E2E-19
E2E-21	lemma	drafting	Bounded factor partition and heavy class	Factor $$B=c\\prod_{i=1}^s C_i$$ into distinct irreducibles and set $\\Delta_i=\\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}C_i$. Primitivity implies that every $C_i$ depends on $Z$, hence $$s\\le\\deg_ZB\\le D/d\\le K_\\theta.$$ Vertical simplicity partitions $G_1$ uniquely into factor classes $G_{1,i}$, preserving minimum degree, with $$C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0,\n\\quad C_i(x,f(x))=0,\n\\quad C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\\ne0.$$ Some class has mass $\\mu_i=e(G_{1,i})\\ge b_\\theta\\epsilon/K_\\theta$, and each of its point vertices has more than $D\\ge\\Delta_i$ factor-consistent incident lines.	E2E-20
E2E-22	lemma	drafting	High-direction factor linearization	Let $C=C_i$ and $\\Delta=\\Delta_i<D<p$. At a point where $C_Z\\ne0$, more than $\\Delta$ compatible degree-at-most-$d$ graph directions determine a polynomial $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$C(X,Y,Q(X,Y))\\equiv0.$$ Since $C$ is irreducible and depends on $Z$, it follows that $$C\\sim Z-Q.$$	E2E-21
E2E-23	reduction	candidate	All heavy-factor edges charge to one polynomial	For every point vertex $x$ of the heavy factor class, $C_i(x,f(x))=0$ and $C_i\\sim Z-Q$ imply $Q(x)=f(x)$. Since $$|E_{1,i}|\\le(p+1)|X_{1,i}|,$$ one has $$|X_{1,i}|/p^2\\ge e(G_{1,i})\\ge b_\\theta\\epsilon/K_\\theta.$$ Combining this with the direct branch of `E2E-15` proves $\\mathsf{Done}_{c_\\theta}$ for $c_\\theta=\\min\\{a_\\theta,b_\\theta/K_\\theta\\}$.	E2E-22
E2E-24	reduction	candidate	Close every fixed exponent	For fixed $\\eta>0$, apply the chain with $\\theta=\\min\\{\\eta,1/4\\}$. The implication $$\\epsilon\\ge C_\\eta\\rho^{1-\\eta}\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\\epsilon\\ge C_\\eta\\rho^{1-\\theta}$$ yields one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial with agreement at least $c_\\eta\\epsilon$.	E2E-23
E2E-25	obligation	refuted	Uniform exact-linear endpoint	There exist absolute constants $C,c>0$ such that every legal instance satisfying $\\epsilon\\ge C d/p$ also satisfies $\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\\ge c\\epsilon$.	E2E-01
E2E-GOAL	theorem	blocked	Near-linear bivariate soundness	For every fixed $\\eta>0$ there exist $C_\\eta,c_\\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, every integer $100<d<p$, and every legal bivariate line table, $$\\operatorname{Agr}_{\\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P)\\ge C_\\eta(d/p)^{1-\\eta}$$ implies that there is one $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\\Pr_x[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge c_\\eta\\operatorname{Agr}_{\\mathrm{LVP}}(f,P).$$	E2E-24
FILE: research_state/campaign-10-ultra/roadmaps/algebraic-engine/round-0002.json
ROUND 2
SUMMARY: Round 2 replaces the weaker relation bound $D\le A_\eta d\epsilon^{-\eta/(1-\eta)}$ by the strict bounded-weight rank-or-decode interface $D\le K_\theta d$. That change makes the irreducible-factor count $s\le D/d$ constant, so the former heavy-factor-versus-cross-factor gap collapses to pigeonhole concentration. The critical relation branch is: extract a squarefree rank-kernel relation, extend it by reached roots, perform characteristic-safe whole-relation cleanup, globally peel to minimum degree greater than $D$, partition into simple factor-pure classes, linearize one factor at a simple high-direction seed, and aggregate its disconnected class without Vinh mixing. The accepted component decoder and selector obstruction are retained off-path; the exact-revise factorwise polar theorem is retained as a fallback. Accepted singular examples show that $C_Z\ne0$ at the seed is indispensable. The main unresolved input is AE-12, with AE-16 and AE-19 still requiring standalone formalization.
CRITICAL: AE-01 -> AE-02 -> AE-03 -> AE-12 -> AE-13 -> AE-14 -> AE-15 -> AE-16 -> AE-17 -> AE-18 -> AE-19 -> AE-20 -> AE-21 -> AE-22
AE-01	definition	candidate	Accepted-incidence instance	Fix a prime $p$, an integer $100<d<p$, a function $f:\\mathbb F_p^2\\to\\mathbb F_p$, and polynomials $P_L\\in\\mathbb F_p[t]$ with $\\deg P_L\\le d$ for every affine line $L$. Let $$\\mathcal I=\\{(x,L):x\\in L\\text{ and }P_L(x)=f(x)\\}.$$ For $E\\subseteq\\mathcal I$, define $e(E)=|E|/[p^2(p+1)]$.	
AE-02	definition	candidate	Weighted restriction map	Give $X,Y,Z$ weights $1,1,d$ and put $$V_D=\\{A\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\\operatorname{wdeg}(A)\\le D\\},\\qquad N_d(D)=\\sum_{j=0}^{\\lfloor D/d\\rfloor}\\binom{D-dj+2}{2}.$$ For an affine parametrization $L(t)$, set $\\rho_L(A)=A(L(t),P_L(t))$. For $R$ a line family, define $$\\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\\longrightarrow\\prod_{M\\in R}\\mathbb F_p[t]_{\\le D},\\qquad A\\longmapsto(\\rho_M(A))_{M\\in R}.$$	AE-01
AE-03	definition	candidate	Rank-reaching certificate	A rank-reaching certificate is $(G,R,D)$ where $G=(X,T,E)$ is an accepted subgraph, $R\\subseteq T$, $d\\le D$, $|R|>D$, and $$\\operatorname{rank}(\\Phi_{R,D})<\\dim V_D.$$ Moreover, every $L\\in T$ satisfies $$\\left|\\{x:(x,L)\\in E,\\ \\exists M\\in R\\setminus\\{L\\}\\text{ with }(x,M)\\in E\\}\\right|>D.$$ Write $e(G)=e(E)$.	AE-02
AE-04	reduction	candidate	Constant-loss core pruning	If $e(\\mathcal I)=\\epsilon$, there is an accepted subgraph of mass at least $\\epsilon/4$ and minimum degree at least $\\lfloor\\epsilon p/100\\rfloor$.	AE-01
AE-05	theorem	candidate	Accepted component decoder	Let $G$ be a connected accepted subgraph of minimum degree $k$, let $t$ be its number of lines, and put $\\tau=t/[p(p+1)]$. If $$k^2\\ge2^{40}dp,\\qquad k^3\\ge2^{40}d\\tau p^2,$$ then there is one $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$\\Pr_{x\\in\\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge\\frac{k}{800p}.$$	AE-02
AE-06	reduction	candidate	Whole-explainer square-root floor	The AE-05 construction has $$|R|=\\Theta\\left(\\frac{\\tau p^2}{k}\\right),\\qquad D=\\Theta\\left(\\sqrt{\\frac{d\\tau p^2}{k}}\\right),\\qquad D<k.$$ Together with $\\tau\\ge k/(4p)$, this forces $k^2=\\Omega(dp)$ and hence cannot reach $k=\\Theta(\\epsilon p)$ below $\\epsilon=\\Omega((d/p)^{1/2})$.	AE-05
AE-07	theorem	candidate	Universal-cover obstruction	For suitable legal instances with $1/(2K)\\le\\epsilon\\le2/K$, every line family reaching half the accepted points of every accepted line has size at least $p/(32\\epsilon)$, although a constant polynomial has agreement at least $\\epsilon/2$.	AE-01
AE-08	reduction	candidate	Uniform-jet rank barrier	Let $s,D\\in\\mathbb Z$ with $s\\ge1$, $d\\le D<p$, and $n>D$. Define $$C_s(D,d)=\\sum_{a+b<s}\\max(D-a-db+1,0).$$ If raw dimension counting gives $N_d(D)>nC_s(D,d)$ and transfer uses $h$ compatible intersections with $sh>D$, then $$h^2>\\frac{dn}{12}.$$	AE-02
AE-09	lemma	candidate	Characteristic-safe separability and resultants	If $B$ is squarefree and primitive in $Z$ with $\\Delta=\\operatorname{wdeg}(B)<pd$, then $\\gcd(B,B_Z)=1$. If coprime $U,V$ have weighted degrees $u,v$ and $Z$-degrees $r,s$, respectively, then the number of graph lines on which both vanish identically is at most $$su+rv-drs.$$	AE-02
AE-10	lemma	candidate	Assigned-factor polar cleanup	Let $B=c\\prod_iC_i$ be primitive and squarefree with $\\operatorname{wdeg}(B)<p$, and assign each graph line to one factor. Put $$\\delta_i=\\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i),\\quad r_i=\\deg_Z(C_i),\\quad\\gamma_i=\\operatorname{wdeg}(C_{i,Z}),$$ and $$\\Xi_i=(r_i-1)\\delta_i+r_i\\gamma_i-dr_i(r_i-1).$$ If $T_i$ is the assigned line class and $b_i$ is its number of identically ramified lines, then $$b_i\\le\\min\\{|T_i|,\\Xi_i\\},\\qquad\\gamma_i\\le\\delta_i-d,\\qquad\\Xi_i\\le\\frac{\\delta_i^2}{d}-\\delta_i.$$ The ramified accepted incidences satisfy $$\\frac{|E_{\\mathrm{ram}}|}{p^2(p+1)}\\le\\sum_i\\frac{\\min\\{|T_i|,\\Xi_i\\}}{p(p+1)}+\\sum_i\\frac{|T_i|\\gamma_i}{p^2(p+1)}.$$	AE-09
AE-11	lemma	candidate	Singular-seed obstruction	For legal choices with $d+1<p$, there is a primitive, squarefree, absolutely irreducible polynomial $$C=XZ+H(Y),\\qquad\\operatorname{wdeg}(C)=d+1,$$ supporting more than $d+1$ compatible degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines through one common lift, but no $Q$ of total degree at most $d$ with $C(X,Y,Q)=0$. At the common lift, $C_Z=0$.	AE-09
AE-12	obligation	open	Bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy	For every fixed $0<\\theta\\le1/4$, there are constants $C_\\theta,a_\\theta>0$ and $K_\\theta\\ge1$ such that, whenever $$e(\\mathcal I)=\\epsilon\\ge C_\\theta\\left(\\frac dp\\right)^{1-\\theta},$$ either one $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $\\Pr[Q=f]\\ge a_\\theta\\epsilon$, or there is a rank-reaching certificate $(G,R,D)$ with $$e(G)\\ge a_\\theta\\epsilon,\\qquad d\\le D\\le K_\\theta d.$$	AE-03
AE-13	lemma	candidate	Near-linear parameter gate	After increasing $C_\\theta$ in AE-12, the relation alternative satisfies $$\\frac{D}{\\epsilon p}\\le\\frac{K_\\theta}{C_\\theta}\\left(\\frac dp\\right)^\\theta\\le\\frac{a_\\theta}{100}.$$ Consequently, $$D<p,\\qquad \\left(\\frac Dp\\right)^2+\\frac{3D}{p}\\le\\frac{a_\\theta\\epsilon}{2}.$$	AE-12
AE-14	lemma	candidate	Squarefree rank-kernel relation	In the relation alternative, there is a nonzero squarefree $A\\in V_D$ with $$\\rho_M(A)=0\\quad(M\\in R),\\qquad A_Z\\ne0.$$ Indeed, take a nonzero kernel element and replace it by the product of its distinct irreducible factors. The inequality $|R|>D$ excludes a $Z$-independent relation, while $\\deg_Z(A)\\le D/d<p$ makes $Z$-dependence characteristic-safe.	AE-13
AE-15	lemma	candidate	Reached-root identity extension	For every line $L$ of $G$, $$\\rho_L(A)=A(L(t),P_L(t))=0.$$ Each reached accepted point is a root of $\\rho_L(A)$ because it also lies on a selected line $M\\in R$ and both labels equal $f$ there. Since $\\deg\\rho_L(A)\\le D$, more than $D$ such roots force the identity.	AE-14
AE-16	lemma	drafting	Global characteristic-safe cleanup	Write $A=HB$, where $H\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient gcd in $Z$. Delete content lines and the degeneracies of a formal directional derivative coprime to $B$; this removes at most $pD^2+D|T|$ accepted edges. Then repeatedly delete vertices of current degree at most $D$. The surviving graph $G_1=(X_1,T_1,E_1)$ satisfies $$e(G_1)\\ge a_\\theta\\epsilon-\\left(\\frac Dp\\right)^2-\\frac{3D}{p}\\ge\\frac{a_\\theta\\epsilon}{2},\\qquad \\min_{v\\in X_1\\sqcup T_1}\\deg_{G_1}(v)>D.$$ Moreover, $$B(L(t),P_L(t))=0\\quad(L\\in T_1),\\qquad B(x,f(x))=0,\\quad B_Z(x,f(x))\\ne0\\quad(x\\in X_1).$$	AE-15
AE-17	lemma	candidate	Simple-root factor-pure partition	Factor the primitive squarefree relation as $B=c\\prod_{i=1}^sC_i$ over $\\mathbb F_p$. Every $C_i$ depends on $Z$, so $$s\\le\\frac{\\operatorname{wdeg}(B)}d\\le\\frac Dd\\le K_\\theta.$$ At every point of $G_1$, exactly one factor vanishes because $B_Z\\ne0$. Every line has exactly one identically vanishing factor, and an edge joins a line and point carrying the same factor. Thus $G_1$ is the disjoint union of factor-pure classes, and every vertex retains its full $G_1$-degree inside its class.	AE-16
AE-18	lemma	candidate	Constant-factor concentration	Some factor $C_i$ with $\\Delta_i=\\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i)$ has a class $G_i=(X_i,T_i,E_i)$ satisfying $$e(E_i)\\ge\\frac{e(G_1)}s\\ge\\frac{a_\\theta\\epsilon}{2K_\\theta},\\qquad \\min_{v\\in X_i\\sqcup T_i}\\deg_{G_i}(v)>D\\ge\\Delta_i.$$	AE-17
AE-19	obligation	drafting	Simple-seed factor linearization	Let $C\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible and $Z$-dependent with $\\Delta=\\operatorname{wdeg}(C)\\le D<p$. Suppose $C(b,\\alpha)=0$, $C_Z(b,\\alpha)\\ne0$, and more than $\\Delta$ distinct affine lines through $b$ carry polynomials $P_L$ of degree at most $d$ such that $$P_L(b)=\\alpha,\\qquad C(L(t),P_L(t))=0.$$ Then there are $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ and $u\\in\\mathbb F_p^\\times$ such that $$C=u(Z-Q).$$ Applied to AE-18, this linearizes $C_i$.	AE-18
AE-20	reduction	candidate	Disconnected factor aggregation	Let $C_i=u(Z-Q)$ be the factor from AE-19. Every $x\\in X_i$ satisfies $Q(x)=f(x)$, including points in different connected components. Since each point lies on at most $p+1$ affine lines, $$\\Pr_{x\\in\\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge\\frac{|X_i|}{p^2}\\ge\\frac{|E_i|}{p^2(p+1)}=e(E_i)\\ge\\frac{a_\\theta\\epsilon}{2K_\\theta}.$$	AE-19
AE-21	theorem	blocked	Conditional fixed-theta algebraic engine	Assuming AE-12 and AE-19, for every fixed $0<\\theta\\le1/4$ there are $C_\\theta,c_\\theta>0$ such that $$\\epsilon\\ge C_\\theta\\left(\\frac dp\\right)^{1-\\theta}$$ implies the existence of one $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\\Pr[Q=f]\\ge c_\\theta\\epsilon,$$ where one may take $c_\\theta=a_\\theta/(2K_\\theta)$.	AE-20
AE-22	theorem	blocked	Near-linear algebraic propagation engine	For every fixed $0<\\eta<1$, there are constants $C_\\eta,c_\\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, every integer $100<d<p$, and every accepted-incidence instance of density $\\epsilon$, $$\\epsilon\\ge C_\\eta\\left(\\frac dp\\right)^{1-\\eta}$$ implies that some $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfies $$\\Pr_{x\\in\\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge c_\\eta\\epsilon.$$	AE-21

ROUND 2
SUMMARY: Round 2 keeps popularity pruning, the accepted component sampler, and its conditional square-root decoder as a baseline. The accepted integral-cover obstruction and the revise-grade fractional-cover and collision identities rule out specific density-only selector or pair-energy shortcuts, not soundness or every graph-based argument. The near-linear spine asks instead for a label-aware rank-or-decode configuration of mass $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ and integer weight $D=O_\theta(d)$, without requiring a whole-line restriction. The parameter gate makes $D/p=O_\theta(d/p)$ a small fraction of $\epsilon$; rank extraction, reached-root extension, primitive vertical cleanup, and global degree-$D$ peeling then preserve constant mass. Simple roots partition the survivor into at most $D/d=O_\theta(1)$ factor classes while preserving degrees. A standalone seed-point lift would force one heavy factor to be $Z-Q$, after which all disconnected components of that factor aggregate to the same global $Q$ without Vinh mixing. The principal research bottlenecks are the bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy and factor linearization; the intervening drafting reductions also require exact audits and do not constitute verified progress.
CRITICAL: IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH -> IE-D-RANK-REACH -> IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY -> IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE -> IE-L-RANK-INTERP -> IE-L-RANK-EXTEND -> IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE -> IE-L-VERTICAL-SEPARABLE -> IE-L-WEIGHTED-RAMIFICATION -> IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT -> IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN -> IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE -> IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT -> IE-R-FACTOR-AGGREGATE -> IE-R-CLOSE -> IE-GOAL
IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH	definition	candidate	Accepted-incidence graph	Fix a prime $p$, an integer $100<d<p$, a function $f:\\mathbb F_p^2\\to\\mathbb F_p$, and the affine-line set $\\mathcal L_p$. For each $L\\in\\mathcal L_p$, fix a bijective affine parametrization $L:\\mathbb F_p\\to L$ and a polynomial $P_L\\in\\mathbb F_p[t]$ of degree at most $d$. Define $$E_{\\rm acc}=\\{(L(t),L):L\\in\\mathcal L_p,\\ t\\in\\mathbb F_p,\\ P_L(t)=f(L(t))\\},\\qquad \\epsilon=\\frac{|E_{\\rm acc}|}{p^2(p+1)},$$ and $$\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)=\\max_{\\deg_{\\rm tot}Q\\le d}\\Pr_{x\\in\\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)].$$ For $G=(X,T,E)\\subseteq E_{\\rm acc}$ put $$e(G)=\\frac{|E|}{p^2(p+1)},\\quad \\mu(G)=\\frac{|X|}{p^2},\\quad \\tau(G)=\\frac{|T|}{p(p+1)},\\quad k(G)=\\operatorname{mindeg}(G).$$	
IE-D-RANK-REACH	definition	candidate	Rank-reaching structured family	For an integer $D$ with $d\\le D<p$, let $$V_D=\\{A\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]:\\operatorname{wdeg}_{(1,1,d)}A\\le D\\},\\qquad N_d(D)=\\dim V_D,$$ and $$\\Phi_{R,D}:V_D\\to\\bigoplus_{M\\in R}\\mathbb F_p[t]_{\\le D},\\qquad A\\mapsto\\bigl(A(M(t),P_M(t))\\bigr)_{M\\in R}.$$ For $G=(X,T,E)$ define $$N_G(L)=\\{x:(x,L)\\in E\\},$$ $$\\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)=\\{x\\in N_G(L):\\exists M\\in R\\setminus\\{L\\},\\ x\\in N_G(M)\\}.$$ The tuple $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching if $R\\subseteq T$, $|R|>D$, $\\operatorname{rank}\\Phi_{R,D}<N_d(D)$, and $|\\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>D$ for every $L\\in T$.	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
IE-L-POPULAR-CORE	lemma	candidate	Popularity pruning	If an accepted carrier $G$ has $e(G)=\\delta>0$ and $\\tau(G)\\le\\sigma$, then, for $k_0=\\lfloor\\delta p/100\\rfloor$, it contains $G_0$ with $$e(G_0)\\ge\\delta/4,\\qquad k(G_0)\\ge k_0,\\qquad \\delta/4\\le\\tau(G_0)\\le\\sigma.$$	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
IE-L-COMP-MEASURE	lemma	candidate	Accepted component concentration	Let $G$ be a connected accepted graph with minimum degree $k$, line measure $\\tau$, and $C_*=2^{40}$. If $$k^2\\ge C_*dp,\\qquad k^3\\ge C_*d\\tau p^2,$$ then $$\\mu(G)\\ge\\frac{k}{4p},\\qquad \\tau\\ge\\frac{k}{4p}.$$	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE	lemma	candidate	Component-sensitive reaching sample	Under `IE-L-COMP-MEASURE`, writing $t=|T|$, there exists $R\\subseteq T$ such that $$\\frac{4t}{k}\\le |R|\\le\\frac{16t}{k}$$ and $$|\\operatorname{Reach}_R^G(L)|>\\frac12\\deg_G(L)$$ for every $L\\in T$. Hence $|R|=\\Theta(\\tau p^2/k)$.	IE-L-COMP-MEASURE
IE-T-COMP-DECODE	theorem	candidate	Component-sensitive decoder	If a connected accepted graph of minimum degree $k$ and line measure $\\tau$ satisfies $$k^2\\ge2^{40}dp,\\qquad k^3\\ge2^{40}d\\tau p^2,$$ then there is one $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\\ge\\frac{k}{800p}.$$	IE-L-COMP-SAMPLE
IE-T-SQRT-MILESTONE	theorem	candidate	Conditional square-root milestone	Let $\\Lambda\\ge1$, and let $G_0$ be a popularity core with minimum degree $\\lfloor\\epsilon p/100\\rfloor$. If some connected component of $G_0$ has line measure at most $\\Lambda\\epsilon$ and $$\\epsilon\\ge\\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000\\cdot2^{40}\\Lambda}\\left(\\frac dp\\right)^{1/2},$$ then one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial satisfies $$\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\\ge\\frac{\\epsilon}{160000}.$$	IE-L-POPULAR-CORE,IE-T-COMP-DECODE
IE-R-SQRT-CEILING	reduction	candidate	Support-localization ceiling	If $$0<k\\le C\\epsilon p,\\qquad \\tau\\ge k/(4p),\\qquad k^3\\ge C_*d\\tau p^2,$$ then $$\\epsilon^2\\ge\\frac{C_*}{4C^2}\\frac dp.$$	IE-L-COMP-MEASURE
IE-L-COVER-OBSTRUCTION	lemma	candidate	Universal integral-cover obstruction	For $p\\ge103$ and $2\\le K\\le p/(100\\log p)$ there is a legal table with $$\\frac1{2K}\\le\\epsilon\\le\\frac2K$$ such that every $R$ reaching half of every accepted line satisfies $$|R|\\ge\\frac{Kp}{16}\\ge\\frac{p}{32\\epsilon},$$ while some constant polynomial has agreement at least $\\epsilon/2$.	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
IE-L-FRACTIONAL-COVER-OBSTRUCTION	lemma	candidate	Regular fractional-cover obstruction	There is a legal full-line-support table with $\\epsilon=d/p$, every line of accepted degree $d$, every point of accepted degree $d$ or $d+1$, and global decoder $Q=0$. Put $N(L)=\\{x:(x,L)\\in E_{\\rm acc}\\}$. For weights $\\lambda_M\\ge0$, require for every line $L$ that $$\\sum_{x\\in N(L)}\\min\\left\\{1,\\sum_{\\substack{M\\ne L\\\\x\\in N(M)}}\\lambda_M\\right\\}\\ge\\frac d2.$$ The minimum total weight $W=\\sum_M\\lambda_M$ satisfies $$\\frac{p(p+1)}{2(d+1)}\\le W\\le\\frac{p(p+1)}{2(d-1)}=\\Theta\\left(\\frac p\\epsilon\\right).$$	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
IE-L-COLLISION-IDENTITY	lemma	candidate	Exact two-line concentration identity	Let $a_x=|\\{L:(x,L)\\in E_{\\rm acc}\\}|$ and $$\\Gamma_2=\\frac{1}{p^3(p+1)}\\sum_x a_x(a_x-1).$$ Then $$\\Gamma_2=\\epsilon^2-\\frac{\\epsilon(1-\\epsilon)}p+\\frac{\\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)}.$$ In particular, $\\Gamma_2\\ge\\epsilon^2/2$ when $\\epsilon\\ge2/p$. The variance-free formula is attained by accepting $k$ direction classes, where $\\epsilon=k/(p+1)$ and $Q=0$.	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
IE-X-EXACT-LINEAR	theorem	refuted	Exact-linear endpoint	The assertion that there exist absolute $C,c>0$ such that every legal instance satisfies $$\\epsilon\\ge C\\frac dp\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\\ge c\\epsilon$$ is false, already for $d=101$.	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH
IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY	obligation	open	Bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy	For every fixed $0<\\theta\\le1/4$ there exist $C_\\theta\\ge1$, $0<a_\\theta\\le1$, and $K_\\theta\\ge1$ such that, if $$\\epsilon\\ge C_\\theta\\left(\\frac dp\\right)^{1-\\theta},$$ then either $$\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\\ge a_\\theta\\epsilon,$$ or there exist an accepted subgraph $G=(X,T,E)$, a set $R\\subseteq T$, and an integer $D$ such that $$e(G)\\ge a_\\theta\\epsilon,\\qquad d\\le D\\le K_\\theta d,$$ and $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching.	IE-D-RANK-REACH
IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE	reduction	candidate	Near-lossless parameter gate	After enlarging $C_\\theta$ so that $C_\\theta\\ge16K_\\theta/a_\\theta$, the structured branch satisfies $$\\frac{D}{\\epsilon p}\\le\\frac{K_\\theta}{C_\\theta}\\le\\frac{a_\\theta}{16},\\qquad D\\le\\frac{a_\\theta\\epsilon p}{16}<p.$$ Indeed, for $\\rho=d/p<1$, one has $\\rho=\\rho^{1-\\theta}\\rho^\\theta\\le\\epsilon/C_\\theta$.	IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY
IE-L-RANK-INTERP	lemma	drafting	Low-rank squarefree interpolation	In the structured branch there exists a nonzero squarefree $A\\in V_D$ such that $$A_Z\\not\\equiv0,\n\\qquad A(M(t),P_M(t))\\equiv0$$ for every $M\\in R$.	IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE

IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE	lemma	drafting	Simple-root factor partition	Factor $$B=c\\prod_{i=1}^s C_i$$ into distinct irreducibles and put $\\Delta_i=\\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i)$. Primitivity makes every $C_i$ depend on $Z$, so $$s\\le\\deg_ZB\\le D/d\\le K_\\theta.$$ At a simple lifted point exactly one factor vanishes; every incident line has that same factor identity. Hence the simple-root graph is a disjoint union of factor classes $F_i$, each preserving every vertex degree. For some $i$, $$e(F_i)\\ge\\frac{e(G_1)}s\\ge\\frac{a_\\theta}{2K_\\theta}\\epsilon,\\qquad k(F_i)>D\\ge\\Delta_i,$$ and its vertices satisfy $$C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$$ on every line and $$C_i(x,f(x))=0,\\qquad C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\\ne0$$ at every point. If a nonempty accepted factor class $F$ has $k(F)>D$, satisfies $C(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$ on every line vertex, and has $C_Z(x,f(x))\\ne0$ at every point vertex, then there exists $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$C\\sim Z-Q.$$	IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE

ROUND 2
SUMMARY: Round 2 preserves the exact accepted KTZ component chain `E2E-04`–`E2E-12`, whose available unconditional machinery stops at the square-root frontier. The near-linear spine is `E2E-15`–`E2E-24`: an algebra-aware rank-or-decode dichotomy must retain $\Omega_\theta(\epsilon)$ mass while producing weighted degree $D=O_\theta(d)$. That bound makes the accepted whole-relation cleanup loss $O_\theta(d/p)$ absorbable and leaves only $O_\theta(1)$ irreducible factors. A heavy simple factor then linearizes as $Z-Q$, and all of its edges—across disconnected components—charge to one global polynomial without Vinh mixing. The factor lift is mathematically supported but lacks a fresh exact-accepted standalone source. Accepted round-2 singular-pencil examples show that its simple-root hypothesis cannot be weakened to absolute irreducibility, smooth resultants, or high direction count alone. The goal remains blocked on the low-weight incidence dichotomy and exact audits of the strengthened algebraic transfers; the uniform $Cd/p$ endpoint remains separately refuted.

If some connected component of $G_0$ has line measure at most $\\Lambda\\epsilon$ and $$\\epsilon\\ge\\sqrt{8{,}000{,}000\\cdot2^{40}\\Lambda}\\left(\\frac dp\\right)^{1/2},$$ then one total-degree-at-most-$d$ polynomial satisfies $$\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\\ge\\frac{\\epsilon}{160000}.$$	IE-L-POPULAR-CORE,IE-T-COMP-DECODE	The existence of the required small-line-measure component remains an antecedent, not a proved localization theorem.
IE-R-SQRT-CEILING	reduction	candidate	Support-localization ceiling	If $$0<k\\le C\\epsilon p,\\qquad \\tau\\ge k/(4p),\\qquad k^3\\ge C_*d\\tau p^2,$$ then $$\\epsilon^2\\ge\\frac{C_*}{4C^2}\\frac dp.$$	IE-L-COMP-MEASURE	Thus smaller component support alone cannot move the accepted cardinality/root-budget architecture beyond the square-root scale.
IE-L-COVER-OBSTRUCTION	lemma	candidate	Universal integral-cover obstruction	For $p\\ge103$ and $2\\le K\\le p/(100\\log p)$ there is a legal table with $$\\frac1{2K}\\le\\epsilon\\le\\frac2K$$ such that every $R$ reaching half of every accepted line satisfies $$|R|\\ge\\frac{Kp}{16}\\ge\\frac{p}{32\\epsilon},$$ while some constant polynomial has agreement at least $\\epsilon/2$.	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH	This rules out a specific graph-only sparsification principle, not soundness: the same table already has an immediate decoder.
IE-L-FRACTIONAL-COVER-OBSTRUCTION	lemma	candidate	Regular fractional-cover obstruction	There is a legal full-line-support table with $\\epsilon=d/p$, every line of accepted degree $d$, every point of accepted degree $d$ or $d+1$, and global decoder $Q=0$. Put $N(L)=\\{x:(x,L)\\in E_{\\rm acc}\\}$. For weights $\\lambda_M\\ge0$, require for every line $L$ that $$\\sum_{x\\in N(L)}\\min\\left\\{1,\\sum_{\\substack{M\\ne L\\\\x\\in N(M)}}\\lambda_M\\right\\}\\ge\\frac d2.$$ The minimum total weight $W=\\sum_M\\lambda_M$ satisfies $$\\frac{p(p+1)}{2(d+1)}\\le W\\le\\frac{p(p+1)}{2(d-1)}=\\Theta\\left(\\frac p\\epsilon\\right).$$	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH	The source audit verdict is `revise`; this is a diagnostic node outside the goal ancestry.
IE-L-COLLISION-IDENTITY	lemma	candidate	Exact two-line concentration identity	Let $a_x=|\\{L:(x,L)\\in E_{\\rm acc}\\}|$ and $$\\Gamma_2=\\frac{1}{p^3(p+1)}\\sum_x a_x(a_x-1).$$ Then $$\\Gamma_2=\\epsilon^2-\\frac{\\epsilon(1-\\epsilon)}p+\\frac{\\operatorname{Var}_x(a_x)}{p(p+1)}.$$ In particular, $\\Gamma_2\\ge\\epsilon^2/2$ when $\\epsilon\\ge2/p$. The variance-free formula is attained by accepting $k$ direction classes, where $\\epsilon=k/(p+1)$ and $Q=0$.	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH	This identifies accepted-degree variance as the correction to the random two-line baseline. The source audit verdict is `revise`.
IE-X-EXACT-LINEAR	theorem	refuted	Exact-linear endpoint	The assertion that there exist absolute $C,c>0$ such that every legal instance satisfies $$\\epsilon\\ge C\\frac dp\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\\ge c\\epsilon$$ is false, already for $d=101$.	IE-D-ACCEPTED-GRAPH	The modal construction refutes this uniform endpoint but remains below every fixed $(d/p)^{1-\\eta}$ trigger. The source audit verdict is `revise`.
IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY	obligation	open	Bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy	For every fixed $0<\\theta\\le1/4$ there exist $C_\\theta\\ge1$, $0<a_\\theta\\le1$, and $K_\\theta\\ge1$ such that, if $$\\epsilon\\ge C_\\theta\\left(\\frac dp\\right)^{1-\\theta},$$ then either $$\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\\ge a_\\theta\\epsilon,$$ or there exist an accepted subgraph $G=(X,T,E)$, a set $R\\subseteq T$, and an integer $D$ such that $$e(G)\\ge a_\\theta\\epsilon,\\qquad d\\le D\\le K_\\theta d,$$ and $(G,R,D)$ is rank-reaching.	IE-D-RANK-REACH	This is the principal incidence obligation. A proof may start with `IE-L-POPULAR-CORE`, but the sufficient conclusion is intentionally mass-only. Both cover obstructions explain why the decoder alternative is essential.
IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE	reduction	candidate	Near-lossless parameter gate	After enlarging $C_\\theta$ so that $C_\\theta\\ge16K_\\theta/a_\\theta$, the structured branch satisfies $$\\frac{D}{\\epsilon p}\\le\\frac{K_\\theta}{C_\\theta}\\le\\frac{a_\\theta}{16},\\qquad D\\le\\frac{a_\\theta\\epsilon p}{16}<p.$$ Indeed, for $\\rho=d/p<1$, one has $\\rho=\\rho^{1-\\theta}\\rho^\\theta\\le\\epsilon/C_\\theta$.	IE-O-LOW-WEIGHT-DICHOTOMY	The constant $C_\\theta$ may be enlarged without weakening the dichotomy. No characteristic-size assumption beyond $d<p$ is introduced.
IE-L-RANK-INTERP	lemma	drafting	Low-rank squarefree interpolation	In the structured branch there exists a nonzero squarefree $A\\in V_D$ such that $$A_Z\\not\\equiv0,\n\\qquad A(M(t),P_M(t))\\equiv0$$ for every $M\\in R$.	IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-GATE	Choose a nonzero kernel element and replace it by its squarefree radical. More than $D$ sampled affine lines exclude a $Z$-independent radical; $D<p$ then makes its $Z$-derivative nonzero.
IE-L-RANK-EXTEND	lemma	candidate	Reached-root extension	For every line vertex $L$ of $G$, the more than $D$ distinct reached accepted points force $$A(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0.$$	IE-L-RANK-INTERP	The cited step supplies the accepted root-counting template; the rank-producing hypothesis is new upstream.
IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE	lemma	drafting	Primitive content reduction	Write $A=HB$, where $H\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ is the coefficient content, $B$ is primitive in $Z$, $h=\\deg H$, and $\\delta=\\operatorname{wdeg}(B)$. Then $B$ is squarefree and $Z$-dependent, $$d\\le\\delta,\\qquad h+\\delta\\le D.$$ At most $h$ affine lines satisfy $H(L(t))\\equiv0$; every other line vertex of $G$ satisfies $$B(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0.$$	IE-L-RANK-EXTEND	This separates content removal from the prime-field vertical-separability input.
IE-L-VERTICAL-SEPARABLE	lemma	candidate	Prime-field vertical separability	Under `IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE`, $$\\gcd(B,B_Z)=1.$$	IE-L-PRIMITIVE-REDUCE	Here $\\delta\\le D<p<dp$, which is inside the exact separability range of the cited lemma.
IE-L-WEIGHTED-RAMIFICATION	lemma	candidate	Weighted ramification-line bound	Let $r=\\deg_ZB$. The number of degree-at-most-$d$ graph lines satisfying both $$B(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0,\\qquad B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$$ is at most $$(2r-1)\\delta-dr^2\\le\\delta^2/d-\\delta.$$	IE-L-VERTICAL-SEPARABLE	This is the weighted-resultant estimate applied to the coprime pair $B,B_Z$.
IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT	lemma	drafting	Prime-field vertical cleanup count	At most $$h+\\delta^2/d-\\delta\\le D^2/d-D$$ lines are content-trivial or identically $B_Z$-ramified. On every other line, $B(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$, while the nonzero polynomial $B_Z(L(t),P_L(t))$ has at most $\\delta-d\\le D-d$ roots. Thus deleting all nonsimple accepted incidences costs at most $$(D^2/d-D)/(p(p+1))+(D-d)/p\\le D^2/p^2+D/p.$$	IE-L-WEIGHTED-RAMIFICATION	This sharpens the earlier generic exceptional-edge count and avoids directional-derivative transfer. The formula is written without fraction commands to prevent control-character ambiguity.
IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN	reduction	drafting	Near-lossless global cleanup	After the vertical deletion, iteratively delete every point or line vertex of current degree at most $D$. Peeling costs less than $$\\frac{D(|\\mathbb F_p^2|+|\\mathcal L_p|)}{p^2(p+1)}<\\frac{2D}{p}.$$ There remains a nonempty graph $G_1$ satisfying $$e(G_1)\\ge\\frac{a_\\theta}{2}\\epsilon,\\qquad k(G_1)>D,$$ with $B(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$ on every line vertex and $$B(x,f(x))=0,\\qquad B_Z(x,f(x))\\ne0$$ at every point vertex.	IE-L-CLEAN-COUNT	Each peeling deletion is charged once to the vertex removed at that stage. The cleanup and peeling costs together are at most $4D/p\\le a_\\theta\\epsilon/4$.
IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE	lemma	drafting	Simple-root factor partition	Factor $$B=c\\prod_{i=1}^s C_i$$ into distinct irreducibles and put $\\Delta_i=\\operatorname{wdeg}(C_i)$. Primitivity makes every $C_i$ depend on $Z$, so $$s\\le\\deg_ZB\\le D/d\\le K_\\theta.$$ At a simple lifted point exactly one factor vanishes; every incident line has that same factor identity. Hence the simple-root graph is a disjoint union of factor classes $F_i$, each preserving every vertex degree. For some $i$, $$e(F_i)\\ge\\frac{e(G_1)}s\\ge\\frac{a_\\theta}{2K_\\theta}\\epsilon,\\qquad k(F_i)>D\\ge\\Delta_i,$$ and its vertices satisfy $$C_i(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$$ on every line and $$C_i(x,f(x))=0,\\qquad C_{i,Z}(x,f(x))\\ne0$$ at every point.	IE-R-LOW-WEIGHT-CLEAN	The cited step supplies factorwise $Z$-dependence; its audit verdict is `revise`. The degree-preserving star partition is a separate drafting argument.
IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT	obligation	drafting	Seed-point factor linearization	Let $C\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y,Z]$ be irreducible with $\\Delta=\\operatorname{wdeg}(C)\\le D<p$. If a nonempty accepted factor class $F$ has $k(F)>D$, satisfies $C(L(t),P_L(t))\\equiv0$ on every line vertex, and has $C_Z(x,f(x))\\ne0$ at every point vertex, then there exists $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ such that $$C\\sim Z-Q.$$	IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE	At a simple point, use the formal implicit root. More than $\\Delta$ compatible directions kill its homogeneous terms from degree $d+1$ through $\\Delta$; the resulting degree-$d$ truncation annihilates $C$, and irreducibility should force association. A fresh exact audit is required.
IE-R-FACTOR-AGGREGATE	reduction	candidate	Global factor aggregation	For the factor class $F$ from `IE-L-FACTOR-CONCENTRATE`, the identities $C(x,f(x))=0$ and $C\\sim Z-Q$ imply $Q(x)=f(x)$ at every point vertex of $F$. Since every affine point lies on at most $p+1$ lines, $$\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\\ge\\frac{|X(F)|}{p^2}\\ge e(F)\\ge\\frac{a_\\theta}{2K_\\theta}\\epsilon.$$	IE-L-FACTOR-LIFT	Association to one fixed irreducible factor aggregates all disconnected factor components without point-line mixing or Vinh incidence estimates.
IE-R-CLOSE	reduction	blocked	Close the direct and structured branches	For arbitrary fixed $\\eta>0$, set $\\theta=\\min\\{\\eta,1/4\\}$. Since $0<d/p<1$ and $$(d/p)^{1-\\eta}\\ge(d/p)^{1-\\theta},$$ the dichotomy and factor aggregation yield constants $C_\\eta,c_\\eta>0$ such that $$\\epsilon\\ge C_\\eta(d/p)^{1-\\eta}\\quad\\Longrightarrow\\quad\\operatorname{Agr}_d(f)\\ge c_\\eta\\epsilon.$$	IE-R-FACTOR-AGGREGATE	The direct branch is terminal. The structured branch loses only a $\\theta$-dependent constant because $D/d$ is bounded.
IE-GOAL	theorem	blocked	Near-linear bivariate line-versus-point soundness	For every fixed $\\eta>0$ there exist $C_\\eta,c_\\eta>0$ such that, for every prime $p$, integer $100<d<p$, function $f:\\mathbb F_p^2\\to\\mathbb F_p$, and degree-at-most-$d$ affine-line table, $$\\epsilon\\ge C_\\eta\\left(\\frac dp\\right)^{1-\\eta}$$ implies the existence of one $Q\\in\\mathbb F_p[X,Y]$ of total degree at most $d$ satisfying $$\\Pr_{x\\in\\mathbb F_p^2}[Q(x)=f(x)]\\ge c_\\eta\\epsilon.$$	IE-R-CLOSE	The principal bottlenecks are the bounded-weight rank-or-decode dichotomy and an exact factor-lift proof. Intermediate drafting reductions also require exact audits; work states assert only activity.
